79:["$","h3",null,{"id":"running-a-line-halt-and-an-interrupt-on-a6","className":"x:tracking-tight x:text-slate-900 x:dark:text-slate-100 x:font-semibold x:target:animate-[fade-in_1.5s] x:mt-8 x:text-2xl","children":[["Running a line: ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"HALT"}]," and an interrupt on A6"],["$","$L23",null,{"id":"running-a-line-halt-and-an-interrupt-on-a6"}]]}]
7a:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":["Here is the clever part. When the CPU reaches the ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$76"}]," at the end of a line, it executes ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"HALT"}]," and\nstops. A halted Z80 does not quite stop, though: it keeps executing internal ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"NOP"}],"s, one M1 cycle\nevery 4 T-states, and ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"each M1 still increments R and puts it on the address bus"}]," during refresh."]}]
7b:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":["On the ZX81, the Z80’s ",["$","strong",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"INT"}]," pin is wired to address line A6"]}],". While the refresh address is on the\nbus, A6 carries ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"bit 6 of R"}],". So the moment R’s bit 6 goes to 0, an interrupt is requested."]}]
7c:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":["But wait: A6 is just as often 0 at other moments too, for example while an opcode is fetched from an\naddress with bit 6 clear. Why does that not cause an interrupt? Because the Z80 does not watch INT\nall the time. As ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://www.zilog.com/docs/z80/um0080.pdf","target":"_blank","rel":"noreferrer","className":"x:focus-visible:nextra-focus x:text-primary-600 x:underline x:hover:no-underline x:decoration-from-font x:[text-underline-position:from-font]","children":["Zilog’s manual",[" ",["$","svg",null,{"fill":"none","stroke":"currentColor","strokeLinecap":"round","strokeLinejoin":"round","strokeWidth":1.7,"viewBox":"0 0 24 24","height":"1em","className":"x:inline x:align-baseline x:shrink-0","children":[["$","path",null,{"d":"M7 17L17 7"}],["$","path",null,{"d":"M7 7h10v10"}]]}]]]}]," puts it, the CPU\n“samples the interrupt signal (INT) with the rising edge of the final clock at the end of any\ninstruction”. While interrupts are on, the display runs only instructions that end with the refresh\nof their M1 cycle (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"EI"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"JP (HL)"}],", the stolen ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"NOP"}],"s and the ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"NOP"}],"s inside ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"HALT"}],"). So at that one\nmoment the address bus carries the refresh address, and A6 is bit 6 of R, not of PC. Everywhere else\ninterrupts are simply switched off: the CPU ignores INT unless they have been enabled, and only the\ndisplay code enables them, just before it runs a line."]}]
7d:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":["R is a counter of M1 cycles, and the ROM can load it with ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"LD R,A"}],". So ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"R is a countdown timer"}],":\nload it with the right value, and the interrupt arrives after exactly the right number of opcode\nfetches. The ROM loads ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$DD"}]," into R before each line. Counting from there, the line’s 32 characters\nand its ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"HALT"}]," take R up to ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$FF"}],". The next M1, the first one inside the ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"HALT"}],", refreshes with R\n= ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$80"}],": bit 6 is now 0, and ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"INT"}]," fires."]}]
7e:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":["Why ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$80"}]," and not ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$00"}],"? Because the Z80 increments only the ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"lower 7 bits"}]," of R, and ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"bit 7 never\nchanges"}]," when R counts: it keeps whatever ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"LD R,A"}]," put there. So from ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$FF"}]," (bit 7 set, lower bits\n",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"111 1111"}],") the lower 7 bits wrap round to ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"000 0000"}],", while bit 7 stays 1, and R becomes ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$80"}],". 91:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Short lines and empty lines."}]," The table assumes a full line of 32 characters. b1:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"3. 146:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":["Why does the screen go blank while a BASIC program runs in FAST mode, rather than just freezing? The\nanswer is VSYNC, which gets ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"stuck on"}],". As ",["$","a",null,{"href":"https://k1.spdns.de/Vintage/Sinclair/80/Sinclair%20ZX80/Tech%20specs/Wilf%20Rigter's%20ZX81%20Video%20Display%20Info.htm","target":"_blank","rel":"noreferrer","className":"x:focus-visible:nextra-focus x:text-primary-600 x:underline x:hover:no-underline x:decoration-from-font x:[text-underline-position:from-font]","children":["Wilf Rigter describes the ZX81 video\nhardware",[" ",["$","svg",null,{"fill":"none","stroke":"currentColor","strokeLinecap":"round","strokeLinejoin":"round","strokeWidth":1.7,"viewBox":"0 0 24 24","height":"1em","className":"x:inline x:align-baseline x:shrink-0","children":[["$","path",null,{"d":"M7 17L17 7"}],["$","path",null,{"d":"M7 7h10v10"}]]}]]]}],",\nan ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"IN"}]," from port ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$FE"}]," pulls the video output down to the sync level and so starts VSYNC, but only\nwhile the NMI generator is off. While VSYNC is on, the ULA also stops making HSYNC, and only an ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"OUT"}],"\nends both. Now, between statements, the BASIC interpreter checks the BREAK key (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"SPACE"}],") with the\nROM’s routine at ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$0F46"}],", which reads the keyboard with ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"IN A,($FE)"}]," and never follows it with an\n",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"OUT"}],". In SLOW mode that ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"IN"}]," is harmless, because the NMI generator is on. In FAST mode it is off, so\nthe first BREAK check starts VSYNC and nothing ends it. The television receives a flat sync level, with\nneither HSYNC nor VSYNC to lock onto, and shows a blank screen until the program stops and the ROM\ndraws frames again. What a real set made of that “blank” was usually noise. Klive’s emulated screen is\nblack by default; ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Settings › Emulator › Show TV noise without sync"}]," draws the noise instead. It\nchanges only the display, never the machine or its picture."]}]

14d:["$","p",null,{"className":"x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:leading-7","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":["About ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"EI"}],"."]}]," “No ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"EI"}],"” really means “no ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"EI"}]," unless you know exactly what will happen”. 193:["$","ul",null,{"className":"x:[:is(ol,ul)_&]:my-[.75em] x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:list-disc x:ms-[1.5em]","children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"The upper 32K is for data only."}]," Your program can read and write any byte from ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$8000"}]," to\n",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"$$FFFF"}]," as usual, but ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"code stored there cannot run"}],".
